- **✅ FOLLOW‑UP (2026‑07‑08): glyph‑to‑text gap narrowed from "S+2G" to just "S" (one mini‑square, no extra gaps).** User: the gap (§31I's skip‑one‑square offset, originally derived as `S+2G` = one mini‑square PLUS two inter‑square gaps) reads as too far apart now — narrow it to exactly ONE mini‑square width. `if-stage-head-row` `grid-column-gap` recomputed from the same underlying glyph geometry (mini‑square `S=30` units, gap `G=8` units, out of the wide glyph's 106‑unit viewBox) but using the ratio `S/106=0.283` instead of `(S+2G)/106=0.434`: **`clamp(55.35px,5.985vw,87.225px)` → `clamp(36.08px,3.906vw,56.89px)`**. Because all 4 glyph types were originally calibrated to render their mini‑squares at the SAME physical size for a given viewport (§31/§45 sizing discipline), this ONE shared gap value correctly equals "one mini‑square" for every bar regardless of glyph width — confirmed both families independently compute to the same ~56px physical square size at 1440. Verified (`verify_gapfix.js`, live compiled CSS): `textGapFromGlyph` now 36.1px (430/768) / 56.2px (1440), uniform across all 4 bars, down from 55.3/86.2; glyph‑baseline lock completely unchanged (0/‑0.5/‑17, identical to before); 0 JS errors. Published.

### 45b. Stage‑header glyph/watermark misalignment on eyebrow WRAP — fixed with a small JS correction, not a breakpoint (2026‑07‑08) — shared JS, promoted to `main`. SUPERSEDES the tiny‑breakpoint attempt below.
User reported (with before/after screenshots) that on stage 03 specifically, at the smallest phone width, the glyph and the two text lines were misaligned — user's hypothesis was that it was still aligning to the "belt" under the glyph's mini‑squares. **Root cause was different:** the native CSS baseline‑lock formula (§45) assumes the eyebrow ("STAGE 0X · …") renders as exactly ONE line; at narrow viewports the LONGER stage names wrap to two lines, adding real height the fixed‑formula doesn't know about, so the glyph (positioned by a static `margin-top` calc) ends up too high relative to the now‑lower title. Confirmed by forcing `white-space:nowrap` in a test harness — delta snapped from −17px to −0.5px instantly, isolating the wrap (not the belt) as the cause.
- **⚠️ FIRST ATTEMPT (reverted): a Webflow "tiny" (≤479px) breakpoint override assuming exactly 2 eyebrow lines.** Worked perfectly AT 430px (the user's reported case) but a binary‑search measurement (`find_wrap_threshold.js`) showed **each stage's eyebrow actually wraps at a DIFFERENT width** — stage01 ≤450, stage02 ≤522, stage03 ≤548, stage04 ≤488 — because the four stage names are different lengths. Webflow only offers 3 fixed breakpoints (479/767/991), none of which land near 522 or 548, so a single flat breakpoint override necessarily left a gap: correct at 430, but WRONG (still assuming 1 line, now actually 2) for the longer names in the 480–548px range, and wrong the other way if pushed to the 767 breakpoint instead (stage01 doesn't wrap until 450, so it would be wrongly over‑corrected from 451–767). **No native‑breakpoint‑only solution can satisfy all four simultaneously.** Removed via `update_style` + `remove_properties` on the 4 classes' `tiny` breakpoint (verified compiled CSS shows no tiny override left — pure `main` formula at all widths).
- **REAL FIX — shared JS module `stage-header-eyebrow-fix` (idea-factory.js).** Measures the eyebrow's ACTUAL rendered height at runtime (`getBoundingClientRect().height` vs its own `font-size`, since `line-height:1`); if it's taller than one line (wrapped), nudges `.if-stage-glyphimg`'s `margin-top` and `.if-stage-wm`'s `top` down by exactly the extra height, via inline style layered on top of the native CSS value (clears any prior inline override first so it always re‑reads the CSS‑formula's *current* value, never a stale cache — correct on resize too). Runs on load, `document.fonts.ready`, and debounced `resize`. **Generalizes automatically** — no hardcoded per‑stage widths, works for 1 extra line or 2, adapts instantly if a stage name is ever edited to a different length. This is squarely a "Webflow genuinely can't do this" case (per CLAUDE.md §0/§7): aligning to a sibling's *content‑dependent* wrapped height isn't expressible as a static CSS breakpoint at all, let alone one of Webflow's 3 fixed ones.
- **Verified exhaustively** (`verify_jsfix.js`, live compiled CSS + real Interstate + cropped glyphs, 13 widths from 1440 down to 320): delta = **0 exactly** at every width where any bar has wrapped (450/430/375/320 where ALL 4 wrap; 480–522 range where only the longer‑name stages wrap while shorter ones correctly stay on the native single‑line formula) — better than the native system's own ±0.5–0.8px baseline residual. 0 JS errors at every width. (An unrelated, pre‑existing horizontal‑overflow quirk in footer elements at ≤375px was found during this check — NOT caused by this fix, out of scope, left alone.)
- **Promoted:** `idea-factory.js` → `main` (commit `c521fe6`) → confirmed served by GitHub Pages (byte‑exact). Native Designer change (tiny‑override removal) published same session. `stable` not advanced yet.
- **Lesson for future per‑stage alignment work:** when a CSS lock depends on an assumption about a sibling's line count/wrapping, do NOT reach for a breakpoint override — measure whether the wrap point is content‑length‑dependent first (it almost always is, once labels differ in length). If so, only a small runtime JS correction can be exactly right at every width; a fixed breakpoint can only be exactly right at the ONE width it was tuned for.

## 46. ABOUT synthesis diagram + "how people move through it" — proper responsive STACKING (2026‑07‑08) — native Designer, PUBLISHED
User: the 4‑box "loop, not a line" diagram and the STUDENT/RESEARCHER/COMPANY move‑list "are not stacking responsively at all as the browser frame narrows." Audit confirmed two distinct gaps:
- **Diagram (`if-syn-steps`/`if-syn-step`/`if-syn-glyphimg`/`if-syn-arrow`) had a `small` (≤767) SHRINK override but NOTHING at `medium` (≤991)** — and the desktop sizes (4×~218px steps + 3 arrows + gaps ≈ 1064px) don't fit the ~855px available inside a 991px viewport, so the row was already overflowing/cramped in the whole 768–991 range. Worse, even at `small` it only ever SHRANK the same 4‑across row — it never actually stacked to a mobile‑appropriate vertical layout.
- **Move rows (`if-move-row` = pill + description, flex, no wrap) had ZERO responsive handling at all** — the OUTER `if-move-cols` 2‑column grid already collapsed to 1 column at `medium` (pre‑existing), but each inner pill‑and‑text row stayed side‑by‑side at every width, squeezing the description into an ever‑narrower column with no floor.
- **Fix — diagram now STACKS at `medium` (≤991), replacing the old shrink‑only `small` overrides:** `if-syn-steps` → `flex-direction:column;align-items:center;grid-row-gap:clamp(8px,2vw,16px)`; `if-syn-step` width → `clamp(130px,45vw,220px)`; `if-syn-glyphimg` → `clamp(100px,28vw,170px)` square; `if-syn-arrow` → **`transform:rotate(90deg)`** (points down between stacked boxes instead of sideways) + `margin:6px 0` (was a large one‑sided `margin-top` sized to vertically‑center beside a horizontal row — no longer applicable once stacked) + font-size `clamp(24px,6vw,34px)`. The old `small`‑breakpoint shrink values (tuned for the cramped horizontal squeeze: step/glyph down to 60–96px, stepnum/steplabel font shrunk, arrow margin/font shrunk) were **removed** (`remove_properties`) since the new medium‑and‑down clamps are fluid on their own and the old values would have fought them right at the 767px medium/small seam.
- **Fix — move rows STACK at `small` (≤767):** `if-move-row` → `flex-direction:column;align-items:flex-start;grid-row-gap:8px` (pill sits above its description, both left‑aligned at the same edge). Chosen breakpoint = one step narrower than the diagram's, since `if-move-side` only reaches full page width AFTER `if-move-cols` already went single‑column at `medium` — the pill+description pairing has comfortable room down to phone widths before it needs to stack.
- **Verified** (`verify_stack.js`, live compiled CSS, 10 widths 1440→320): diagram stays a horizontal row at 1440/992 (not yet medium), correctly stacks with rotated arrows at 900/800/767/600/480/430/375/320; move rows stay side‑by‑side through 900/800 (not yet small), correctly stack at 767/600/480/430/375/320; 0 JS errors; 0 page overflow caused by either component at any width (a pre‑existing, unrelated ~14px footer overflow at ≤375 was confirmed via element‑level offender scan to involve zero `if-syn-*`/`if-move-*` elements — not this fix, out of scope). Real screenshots at 900 and 430 confirm a clean vertical diagram (down‑pointing arrows, evenly spaced) and a clean stacked move‑list (pill directly above its text). Designer canvas snapshot of the diagram section confirms the default/desktop view is unaffected (only `medium`/`small` breakpoints were touched).
- **Dials:** diagram stack breakpoint = `medium` (991) on `if-syn-steps`/`if-syn-step`/`if-syn-glyphimg`/`if-syn-arrow`; move‑row stack breakpoint = `small` (767) on `if-move-row`; stacked glyph/step sizing = the clamps above (native Designer, adjust anytime).
- **⚠️ SUPERSEDED same‑day by an unlogged follow‑up revision, then by §47 below.** By the time §47's audit ran, the diagram's actual live state no longer matched this entry at all: `if-syn-step`/`if-syn-glyphimg` had no `medium` override, the `small` override was a **2‑column GRID** (not a 1‑col flex stack), and `if-syn-arrow` used `display:none` at small (not `rotate(90deg)`). Whoever/whatever made that follow‑up change never updated this section — **lesson: if you revise a feature again in the same session, update its CLAUDE.md entry immediately, don't leave the record to drift from reality.** §47 documents the actually‑live state and the further fix on top of it.

## 47. Diagram + move‑pills — container‑relative FILL sizing (not vw‑capped) + full native/embed audit (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, no shared‑file change

User reported (with screenshots, at real Designer‑canvas widths): the "loop, not a line" diagram showed 4 boxes that were **small and boxed in by visible unused side padding even while still 4‑across** ("at TABLET breakpoint … why this odd extra side margin, forcing these boxes to shrink unnecessarily"); the "how people move through it" pills stayed a **tiny fixed chip** far out of proportion to the wide description column beside them; and a Designer‑canvas "this `<script>` embed only displays in preview" banner near a stage photo read as a possible native‑UI violation. Root‑caused and fixed all three; this is the concrete case behind the new HARD RULE #2 bullet above.

- **Root cause (diagram):** `if-syn-step`'s width was `clamp(120px,15.2vw,218px)` — a **viewport‑relative** formula with NO connection to `if-syn-diagram`'s actual available width. At any viewport ≥ ~1050px the vw term already exceeds 218 and the box FREEZES flat at 218px regardless of how much more room the panel has (up to ~60px of dead space at the 1240 cap) — the exact "extra margin forcing shrinkage" the user was pointing at. Below ~1050px the same disconnect runs the other way: the box shrinks with the viewport even where the row still has slack.
- **Fix — container‑relative flex‑fill, main breakpoint (≥992, the "still 4‑across" state):**
  - `if-syn-diagram`: `padding-right`/`padding-left` `clamp(20px,3vw,44px)` → **`clamp(16px,2vw,28px)`** (the wasteful side inset the user flagged, trimmed).
  - `if-syn-step`: removed the fixed `width` clamp entirely; now `flex-grow:1;flex-shrink:1;flex-basis:0%;min-width:0` (compiles to `flex:1`) — the 4 boxes now **equally fill whatever width the row actually has**, continuously, at every viewport.
  - `if-syn-glyphimg`: removed its own independent width/height clamp; now `width:100%;height:auto;aspect-ratio:1 / 1` — the glyph simply fills its OWN step box (square), no second vw formula to fall out of sync with the first.
  - `if-syn-steps`: `align-items:flex-start` → `center` (arrows — a flex sibling of the step columns — now center against the row instead of needing a hand‑tuned `margin-top` that would go stale the moment box height changes with fill‑sizing).
  - `if-syn-arrow`: `margin-top` clamp → `0` (redundant once the parent centers it).
  - `if-syn-steplabel` / `if-syn-stepnum` font‑size bumped ~15% at both `main` and `small` (scaled via the same "multiply floor/coefficient/ceiling by one constant" technique as §31G, so the shared‑window shape is preserved) — closes out the long‑pending "bigger labels" ask now that the boxes themselves are bigger.
  - **Left untouched (already correct):** the `small` (≤767) breakpoint's OWN 2‑column CSS‑GRID override on `if-syn-steps`/`if-syn-step`/`if-syn-glyphimg` (a well‑tuned shared‑window clamp already filling 87–90% of its grid cell at every width 480–767) and `if-syn-arrow{display:none}` at small. This 2×2‑grid‑below‑767 state is what's ACTUALLY live today (see the §46 supersession note above) — §47 only touches the ≥768 flex‑row state.
- **Root cause (pills):** `if-move-pill` was a hard‑coded `width:110px` with zero breakpoint overrides — deliberately sized once to fit "RESEARCHER" at 110px (§31‑B) but with no mechanism to grow when its row had far more than 110px to give, which is exactly what a wide description column beside it created.
- **Fix — pills scale with their row, then flip to full‑width only at true phone width:**
  - `if-move-pill` (main): `width:110px` → **`clamp(110px, 28%, 200px)`** — floor stays the proven‑safe 110px, scales to 28% of the row, capped at 200px so it never balloons absurdly wide once `if-move-cols` drops to 1 column. `font-size:13px` → `clamp(13px,1vw,15px)`.
  - `if-move-pill` at **`tiny`** (≤479, "vertical phone"): `width:100%` — becomes a full‑width block, matching the already‑existing `if-move-row` tiny override (`flex-direction:column`) that stacks pill above description.
  - `move-desc` at **`tiny`**: `margin-bottom:20px` (new) — separates each pill+description pair from the next now that they're stacked full‑width blocks, per the user's explicit ask to keep pairings visually grouped.
- **Root cause (embed scare) — investigated, NOT a violation.** `data_element_tool query_elements` for `type:"HtmlEmbed"` across Home / Students / Faculty / About found exactly **ONE** embed per page, and it is the SAME element (`afd96462‑0b13‑7970‑b66b‑2a7b418e7657`) on every page — the long‑documented, sanctioned `st0` layout embed (§0/§3/§12; hamburger + hero‑grid responsive overrides, genuinely non‑native content, carries the "DO NOT DELETE" banner). `if-stg-img` (the stage photos) queried as plain native `Image` elements, ×4, no embed involved. The Designer's "script embed only displays in preview" banner the user saw is that embed's own placeholder UI floating near the top of a scrolled canvas — not a new violation. Also re‑confirmed (grep of `idea-factory.css`): the file's only 3 `@media` blocks are `prefers-reduced-motion`, and two that mirror Webflow's native 992/991 breakpoint boundaries EXACTLY (containing only `:hover`/`:has()` parent‑child cascades Webflow genuinely cannot compile) — the old custom‑880px Walk‑bar hack and the old custom‑640px card‑grid hack noted in earlier sections (§15/§41) are **both already gone**, migrated to native `medium`/`small` breakpoints (confirmed live via `data_style_tool query_styles` on `if-prog-grid` and `if-walk-item`/`if-walk-row` — both are native‑breakpoint‑driven, no custom numbers). **Nothing to fix here; if a future screenshot shows the card grid failing to collapse, re‑publish and re‑check before assuming a regression — a stale/cached compiled CSS is more likely than an actual reversion.**
- **Verified (offline harness, live published compiled CSS + real Interstate woff2, 22 widths 320→1600):** `fillRatio` (steps' combined width ÷ row width) holds a smooth **84–87%** across the ENTIRE ≥992 flex‑row range with no flat capping at any point (vs. before: flat 218px from ~1050px up, wasting space); the `small`/`tiny` 2×2 grid fills 87–90% of its own cell throughout, unaffected; pills scale continuously 110→200px side‑by‑side then jump to full‑width with a visible 20px grouping gap exactly at the tiny breakpoint; **0px horizontal overflow at every one of the 22 widths**, both before and after the label font‑size bump. Screenshots at 900/767/480/430/375/320 confirm: boxes visibly bigger with the side dead‑space gone, labels bigger and legible (clean 2‑line wrap on "PROVING GROUND"/"ASSEMBLY LINE" at 320px, not overflow), pills properly proportioned beside text, and a clean full‑width stack with grouping gaps below 479px. Published (compiled CSS hashes `e2b44592d` then `718188c29` after the label bump).
- **No shared‑file change** — every fix here is a native Designer `update_style` call (breakpoints `main`/`small`/`tiny`) → `stable` unaffected, nothing to push to `main`/GitHub Pages.
- **Task‑list note:** while auditing, confirmed 4 other previously‑pending tasks (bold‑Georgia‑body rule, synthesis gold bar flush/width, stage‑header glyph‑left restructure, stage‑header baseline‑lock) were ALL already implemented and live from earlier work this session/pre‑compaction, just never marked done — verified each against its documented final state (§31‑A/§32‑A, §33‑B/§40, §45a, §31G/§45/§45b respectively) before marking complete, rather than redoing already‑correct work.

## 48. Walk‑bar label column‑alignment + stage‑header top/bottom margin equalizer (2026‑07‑09) — shared JS only, promoted to `main`

Two more measurement‑based JS fixes, same session as §47, both added to the existing `walk-colalign` and `stage-header-eyebrow-fix` modules' pattern (never a fixed CSS value where the correct value is genuinely runtime‑dependent).

- **A. "WALK THE FACTORY" label left‑aligned to the stacked bar's first‑column text (user ask).** In the stacked (2×2) `if-walk-row` layout, `walk-colalign` already force‑equalizes each column's `.if-walk-txt` width so the two stacked buttons in a column share a left edge (§42) — but that shared edge's actual pixel position depends on the rendered glyph+text width, which is fluid (changes continuously with viewport), so it can't be a fixed CSS padding value. Extended `walk-colalign`: after equalizing column widths, it measures the leftmost column's `.if-walk-txt.getBoundingClientRect().left` relative to the row, and sets `.if-walk-label`'s `padding-left` inline to match; resets to `''` (native CSS) in the one‑row/desktop state. Verified (live‑file harness, 15 widths 320–1400): label‑vs‑column1‑text delta is 0±0.5px at every stacked width; one‑row state's native padding is untouched (delta was never checked there since the module doesn't touch it out of scope).
- **B. Stage‑header top/bottom margin equalizer (user ask, explicitly superseding the §31H "LOCKED — do not touch" note with a new explicit request).** §31H's padding‑top/padding‑bottom split (and §45's ×1.5 rescale of it) was a static formula derived assuming the eyebrow AND the title each render as ONE line — correct for that case, but a title that wraps to 2‑4 lines (varies per stage, per width — the maroon "Where your / idea meets the / real world." stage was the reported case) adds height below the first line that the static formula has no way to know about, so the visual gap above the cluster (glyph+eyebrow+title+watermark) ends up bigger than the gap below it. New module **`stage-head-vcenter`** (placed after `stage-header-eyebrow-fix` so it measures AFTER that module's own eyebrow‑wrap correction is already applied): for each `.if-stage-head-sec`, resets its own `padding-top`/`padding-bottom` to the native CSS value, measures the actual min‑top/max‑bottom across `.if-stage-glyphimg,.if-stage-eyebrow-row,.if-stage-title,.if-stage-wm`, computes `topGap`/`bottomGap` against the section's own box, and redistributes `padding-top −= delta/2; padding-bottom += delta/2` (where `delta = topGap − bottomGap`) — this equalizes the two gaps while leaving their SUM (hence the bar's total height) unchanged, and touches ONLY those two properties, per the user's explicit "just the top and bottom margins, nothing else" scope. Runs on load/fonts‑ready/resize like every other module here.
  - **Verified (live‑file harness, real Interstate + cropped glyph SVGs, 14 widths 320–1440, all 4 stages independently):** `delta` (topGap−bottomGap) is 0 or ±0.3px at every single width/stage combination tested — including widths where different stages wrap to different line counts simultaneously (e.g. at 767px stage 3's section is 451px tall vs stage 1/2/4's 395px, from a longer wrap, yet ALL FOUR still measure topGap=bottomGap to the same tolerance). Screenshot of stage 02 at 500px (title wraps to 4 lines) shows a visually balanced, equal gap above/below the cluster; the glyph‑to‑title baseline lock (§31H/§45's OTHER invariant) is untouched — confirmed the glyph's check‑box bottom row still lands on the title's first line.
- **Promotion note (branch topology):** this session's dev branch (`claude/keen-johnson-f9w833`) and `main` had diverged in commit history (many years of prior direct "Promote: … → GitHub Pages" commits went straight to `main`, never merged back to dev) but their `idea-factory.js` CONTENT was byte‑identical before this change — confirmed via diff before touching anything. Promoted by cherry‑picking the dev‑branch commit onto a branch based on `origin/main`, then pushing that to `main` (clean, no conflicts, since content matched). **If a future session needs to promote a shared‑file change and dev/main have diverged, check content‑identity first (`git show origin/main:<file> | diff - <(git show <base‑commit>:<file>)`) before assuming a straight push or merge is safe.**
- **No native Designer change in this section** — both fixes are pure shared‑JS. Promoted to `main` (commit `9dcdf1b`) → confirmed served by GitHub Pages (65,790 B, byte‑identical to the repo file). `stable` not advanced yet.

## 49. ⚠️⚠️ §48‑B was WRONG to ship JS‑only — corrected to a NATIVE per‑stage clamp formula (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED. §48‑B's JS module is now a belt‑and‑suspenders refinement, not the fix.

**What happened:** §48‑B fixed the stage‑header top/bottom margin asymmetry with JS ONLY. The user opened the Designer (Mobile Portrait breakpoint) and saw the SAME unequal margins the JS was supposed to have fixed — because **the Designer canvas never loads the shared JS file**, so a JS‑only fix is invisible there. This is exactly the anti‑pattern HARD RULE #0 forbids: `padding-top`/`padding-bottom` is a 100% native‑capable property with zero platform‑limitation excuse, so hiding its correction behind code — rather than setting it in the Designer — was the mistake, not a acceptable trade‑off. Restated for the record because the user is (rightly) tired of re‑explaining this: **if a property can be set natively, it MUST be set natively; JS is ONLY for what Webflow genuinely cannot express (§0/§7), and it is never a substitute for verifying/fixing the Designer‑visible state.**

**Root cause of the asymmetry (now fixed correctly):** `if-stage-head-sec`'s padding‑top/‑bottom is ONE continuous fluid formula (`BASE + S − K` / `BASE − S + K`, no per‑breakpoint overrides at all — confirmed via `query_styles`), where `K` was a FLAT constant (`18.8px`, from §45's refit). A flat `K` cannot correct an asymmetry that itself varies continuously with viewport width (measured native‑only, no JS, across 320–1600px: raw delta ranges from **+2.4px at ≤479px to −17.9px at 1440px**, and differs per stage since each title wraps at different widths) — the same "static value where the correct value is genuinely width‑dependent" problem as everywhere else in this file, except this time the fix belonged in the Designer, not in code.

**Fix — made `K` itself a native fluid `clamp()`, calibrated from the SAME raw measurement, then applied per‑stage:**
- Measured the exact per‑stage, per‑width `K` needed to zero the delta (`K_needed = K_old + delta_old/2`, derived from `d(topGap−bottomGap)/dK = −2`) at 11 widths (1440→320) for all 4 stages, native‑only (no JS loaded).
- Two distinct patterns emerged (a 3rd, stage 4, coincidentally fits pattern A well): **Group A** (stage 1, and stage 4 via inheritance) needs `K` from `20.0px` (≤320) down to `12.8px` (≥1440); **Group B** (stages 2 & 3, whose titles wrap to more lines at wide widths) needs `20.0px` down to `9.85px`. Fit each as a native 2‑point linear `clamp()` (same shared‑window technique as HARD RULE #2): Group A → `clamp(12.8px, 22.0571px − 0.64286vw, 20px)`; Group B → `clamp(9.85px, 22.899px − 0.906vw, 20px)`.
- Applied via `update_style`: Group A formula on the BASE `if-stage-head-sec` (covers stage 1 directly, stage 4 by inheritance — no combo override needed, it already fit well); Group B formula as an explicit override on the `if-stage-02` AND `if-stage-03` combo classes (same mechanism as their existing `background-color` override — no `!important` needed, combo‑on‑same‑element naturally wins).
- **⚠️ Caught my own sign error before shipping:** first derivation assumed increasing `K` increases `padding-top` — backwards; the formula is `padding-top = BASE + S − K`, so increasing `K` DECREASES `padding-top`. Verified via a dense offline sweep BEFORE touching Webflow, saw delta blow out to −35.7px (worse, and in a new direction) at 1440, caught the sign flip, corrected, re‑swept clean. **Lesson: always dense‑sweep a derived correction formula offline first — an algebra slip is easy to make and easy to catch this way, before it ever reaches a native style.**
- **Verified (offline, native‑CSS‑only harness — no JS — 23 widths 320–1600, all 4 stages independently):** delta now **0 to ±3.3px** everywhere (worst case at 991–1000px for stages 1/4), vs. the original −17.9 to +2.4px. **Verified in the Designer itself** via `element_snapshot_tool` on the stage‑04 section (per HARD RULE #0 — not a published‑only check): visually balanced top/bottom gap. **Published** (`publish_site`) so the live staging site matches.
- **The §48‑B JS module (`stage-head-vcenter`) was LEFT IN** — it still measures actual rendered gaps and zeroes them at runtime, so it now layers ±0px precision on the published site on top of an already‑good (±3px) native baseline. This is the correct division of labor: **native gets it right where anyone can see it (Designer + published), JS is a refinement for the continuous‑precision case, never the only fix.**

## 50. Synthesis diagram ("loop, not a line") — glyph tiles not top‑aligned when a sibling label wraps (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

At the `small` (≤767) breakpoint, `if-syn-steps` switches from the desktop flex row to a 2‑column CSS grid (`display:grid; grid-template-columns:repeat(2,1fr)`, §46/§47) but never overrode the `align-items:center` inherited from the base flex declaration. Grid `align-items:center` vertically centers each `if-syn-step` (glyph+num+label column) WITHIN its row's cell height — so when one step's label wraps to 2 lines (e.g. "PROVING GROUND") and its row‑mate's stays 1 line (e.g. "BLUEPRINT"), the taller step sets the row height and the shorter one gets centered into it, pushing ITS glyph tile down relative to the taller one's — the two tiles in the same row end up at visibly different heights. **Fix:** `update_style` on `if-syn-steps` at the `small` breakpoint, added `align-items: flex-start` (overrides the inherited `center` only where the grid is active — `main`/desktop flex row is untouched). One property, nothing else touched. **Verified via `element_snapshot_tool`** on the live Designer canvas: both tiles in row 1 (Blueprint/Proving Ground) now share the same top AND bottom edge despite the 1‑line vs 2‑line label difference; same for row 2 (Assembly Line/Scale‑Up). Published.

## 51. Stage‑header cluster — extended the shared transition window to 320px so glyph/text/watermark shrink together, no early freeze (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User (furious, with a Mobile‑Portrait Designer screenshot of stage 04): the glyph was HUGE relative to a 2‑line‑wrapped eyebrow/title, crowding the text — demanded the whole cluster (glyph + 2 lines of text + watermark) shrink together, proportionally, natively, checked in both the Designer and published, "from tablet size down to the smallest responsive size."

- **Root cause: every clamp in the cluster shared window `[360,1440]`** (glyph base/tall/line width+height, dash width+height, eyebrow/title/watermark font‑size, head‑row/head‑txt/eyebrow‑row gaps — verified via `query_styles`, all independently compute floor‑width=360/ceiling‑width=1440 from their own `A + coeff·vw` terms). So the TRANSITION was in sync — but **below 360px everything is simultaneously FROZEN at its floor value**, and §45's ×1.5 "bolder" pass had pushed those floor values up too (glyph‑tall floor 85px→127.5px) without re‑checking them against the ALSO‑floored text at real phone widths (320–375px). Measured: at 320px the glyph (128px) was WIDER than the entire available text column (108px) — the crowding wasn't a transition‑sync bug, it was oversized floor values colliding with a shrinking container below the frozen point.
- **Fix — extended the window to `[320,1440]` for the whole cluster, and reverted the affected MINs to their pre‑§45 (÷1.5) values while keeping every MAX unchanged** (preserves the "bolder at desktop" result from §45 exactly; the ×1.5 pass never touched title/eyebrow/watermark font‑size, so those were left alone — only the pieces actually crowding the text: glyph base/tall/line width+height, dash width+height, head‑row/head‑txt/eyebrow‑row gaps). New floors: glyph base 54px, glyph‑tall/‑line‑width 85px, glyph‑line‑height 36px (box‑row term 24px), dash 24×9px, head‑row gap 24.05px, head‑txt gap 4px, eyebrow‑row gap 12px. Re‑fit each as a native 2‑point `clamp()` on `[320,1440]` (computed in Python to avoid hand‑arithmetic — caught nothing wrong here, but see the sign‑error lesson in §49, same risk on every one of these).
- **The glyph `margin-top` and watermark `top` formulas embed the head‑txt‑gap term** (part of the §31H/45 baseline‑lock "B" sum) — since head‑txt‑gap's min changed, both formulas were rebuilt with the NEW head‑txt‑gap clamp substituted in, keeping the (unchanged) eyebrow‑font and title‑font terms as‑is.
- **Verified (offline, real published compiled CSS + real Interstate + cropped glyph SVGs, all 4 stages, 320–1440px):** side‑by‑side comparison — glyph width now shrinks continuously 201→85px and text‑column width continuously 934→163px (was frozen 128px glyph vs 108px text below 360 before the fix); 0px page overflow at any width; glyph‑to‑title baseline lock (§31H/45) holds at ≈0px everywhere the eyebrow is still one line; §49's vertical‑centering (topGap≈bottomGap) residual stayed small (≤3.9px, down from the original ≤17.9px that motivated §49 — not re‑chased to zero, diminishing returns for a decorative watermark). **Verified in the Designer** via `element_snapshot_tool` on the glyph element directly (renders the correct new proportions; a couple of ancestor‑level snapshots came back missing the glyph/background entirely — traced to the tool's own documented white‑canvas‑only rendering for elements without their own background color, compounded by this glyph's white‑recolored parts being invisible on white, i.e. a snapshot‑tool artifact, not a regression — cross‑checked against the offline harness built from the SAME live published CSS, which is unambiguous). Published; confirmed live (`if-stage-glyphimg`/`if-stage-dash` compiled rules match the new formulas exactly).
- **Known, pre‑existing, UNCHANGED limitation (not introduced by this fix): eyebrow‑text wrapping to 2 lines is still JS‑compensated (`stage-header-eyebrow-fix`, §45b), not native.** Re‑confirmed this is a genuinely different case from §49's mistake — it's a discontinuous STEP (wrap happens at one specific width per stage, abruptly, not a smooth curve) driven by per‑stage‑different label text length, which a `clamp()` cannot represent at all (clamps are continuous). §45b's own conclusion ("no single breakpoint can match all four") still holds under the new sizing. This means the Designer canvas can show a brief, narrow‑range baseline hiccup exactly when an eyebrow wraps (confirmed at stage 4 ≤430px, was ≤488px before this change — new sizing shrank the wrap range, did not worsen it); the published/JS‑enabled site is unaffected. Left as‑is — flagging it here explicitly rather than letting it surface as a surprise later.
- **Dials:** every new floor value above lives in its own native `update_style` call — nudge any of them the same way if the "boldness vs. crowding" balance needs revisiting again. The shared window `[320,1440]` is now the standard for this cluster; if it's ever rebuilt again, keep ALL pieces on the SAME window (this is the HARD RULE #2 "shared transition window" requirement, and the ×1.5 pass — while fine for MAX values — is the reason the MIN values drifted out of proportion this time).

## 52. Synthesis diagram panel — side padding overcorrected too far, restored to match top/bottom (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

§47 had trimmed `if-syn-diagram`'s side padding from `clamp(20,3vw,44)` to `clamp(16,2vw,28)` in response to an earlier "too much side margin" complaint. User (furious): that overcorrected — the side margins ended up SMALLER than both the gaps between glyph boxes and the top/bottom padding, reading as unbalanced. **Fix: set `padding-left`/`padding-right` to the exact same `clamp(24px, 3vw, 44px)` already used for `padding-top`/`padding-bottom`** — uniform padding on all four sides, the simplest, safest way to guarantee a balanced-looking panel at every width without hand-tuning a bespoke value that could just as easily read wrong again. Webflow's own compiled output collapses this to the `padding` shorthand (`padding: clamp(24px, 3vw, 44px)`), confirming all four sides are now identical. Verified via `element_snapshot_tool` (stacked 2×2 state: side margin now visibly comparable to both the inter-box gap and the top padding) and against the live published compiled CSS. Published.

## 53. Stage‑header glyph/text/watermark made eyebrow‑wrap‑independent via native CSS Grid rows (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, JS layer removed as unnecessary for this piece

User (furious, Designer screenshot of stage 03 with a wrapped eyebrow): the glyph's box‑row was floating well above "This is where" instead of locking to it. This is the exact eyebrow‑wrap limitation flagged in §51/§45b as "JS‑compensated, not native" — the user correctly rejected that as insufficient, since it still breaks in the Designer (which never loads the shared JS).

- **Root cause:** the glyph/watermark vertical position was computed by a CSS formula that GUESSES the eyebrow's rendered height (assumes exactly one line). §45b's JS module patched the guess at runtime by measuring the real height — but that only helps the published/JS‑enabled site, never the Designer.
- **Real fix — stop guessing, let the browser's own grid layout measure it.** Restructured `if-stage-head-row` from "2 columns, 1 implicit row (glyph | flex-column-of[eyebrow,title])" to "2 columns × 2 EXPLICIT rows (eyebrow=row1, title=row2)", with `if-stage-head-txt` changed from `display:flex` to **`display:contents`** (dissolves it as a box so its children become direct grid items of `if-stage-head-row`, with zero DOM/element restructuring needed — no `move_element` calls). Glyph is placed at `grid-column:1; grid-row:2; align-self:start` — row 2's top edge is now WHATEVER HEIGHT ROW 1 (the eyebrow) ACTUALLY RENDERS TO, computed natively by the grid engine, 1 line or 2, no formula involved. The margin‑top formula simplifies to just `title's own top‑to‑baseline term − own height term` (drops the eyebrow‑font and head‑txt‑gap terms entirely — they're not needed once the grid handles that offset automatically).
- **Watermark got the same treatment** — confirmed CSS Grid supports `grid-row`/`grid-column` placement on an `position:absolute` item, which scopes its `top`/`right` offsets to THAT GRID AREA instead of the whole container. Prototyped both pieces in isolation first (plain HTML/CSS, forced 1‑line vs 3‑line eyebrow) before touching any real formula — delta (glyph‑bottom vs title‑baseline) came back byte‑identical between the two wrap states, proving the mechanism BEFORE spending time calibrating exact constants.
- **Verified (real compiled CSS + real Interstate + real cropped glyph SVGs, all 4 stages, 320–1440px, natural eyebrow wrap — no forcing):** baseline delta ±0.5px everywhere, completely independent of whether eyebrow renders 1 or 2 lines (confirmed identical across ALL 4 stages simultaneously at every width — the eyebrow‑wrap discontinuity that broke §45b's static formula is structurally gone, not just patched over). Gaps between glyph and text stayed positive (24–57px) at every width, 0 page overflow. Screenshot of stage 03 at 400px (natural 2‑line wrap) shows the box‑row landing exactly on "This is where"'s baseline — the precise scenario from the user's screenshot.
- **The §51 `stage-head-vcenter`/`stage-header-eyebrow-fix` JS modules are now REDUNDANT for this specific alignment** (the thing they were compensating for no longer needs compensating) — left in place since they're harmless no‑ops now (they measure zero deviation and do nothing), but any FUTURE session doing a JS cleanup pass can remove them along with §36's now‑unused `read-line` module.
- **Standing lesson:** when a static CSS formula has to GUESS a sibling's rendered size to position something else, check whether restructuring into CSS Grid (explicit rows/columns, `display:contents` to hoist a wrapper's children, grid‑scoped abs‑pos) lets the BROWSER measure it instead of guessing — this is still 100% native, works identically in the Designer and published, and eliminates the whole class of "JS‑only compensation" bugs rather than patching each width/stage combination.

## 54. Site‑wide audit: hero‑grid, header, and UMD‑bar breakpoints moved from custom‑code embed to native Designer styles (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer + embed cleanup, PUBLISHED

User (furious, About‑page Mobile‑Portrait screenshot of the hero headline overflowing into the image column): demanded a full site audit for responsive behavior still living in custom code instead of native Designer breakpoints, per HARD RULE #2.

- **Read the full contents of the shared `st0` embed** (`afd96462‑0b13‑7970‑b66b‑2a7b418e7657`, same element on all 4 pages, §0/§3/§12) for the first time this session rather than assuming it was untouchable. Found FOUR blocks that were 100% native‑capable with zero platform excuse: a `@media(991px)/(767px)` hero‑grid `grid-template-columns` override, a header tagline/search/burger‑margin visibility block, an unconditional `white-space:nowrap` on the UMD‑bar entity spans, and a UMD‑bar stacking block at custom 500px/380px breakpoints.
- **The hero‑grid bug's real cause was a CSS SPECIFICITY CONFLICT, not a "Designer can't preview code" issue.** `if-hero-grid`'s native "medium" breakpoint had actually been set to `1fr` (single‑column) at some earlier point — but the `if-hero-inner` COMBO class (used on every interior page: About/Students/Faculty) carried an UNCONDITIONAL `grid-template-columns:1.05fr .95fr` with **no breakpoint overrides of its own**. A combo's specificity (0,2,0) beats a plain class (0,1,0) at ANY width regardless of media‑query context, so the combo's fixed 2‑column value was winning at every breakpoint, silently ignoring both the native "medium" value AND the (now‑removed) embed code. The embed's original `!important` was the only reason the OLD code path ever worked at all — `!important` bypasses specificity entirely, which is exactly why the anti‑pattern is hard to notice: it papers over a real gap in the native styles rather than being genuinely unnecessary.
- **Fix:** added the missing `medium`/`small` breakpoint overrides DIRECTLY on the `if-hero-grid.if-hero-inner` combo (`1.15fr .85fr` / `1fr`) — the actual rule governing interior pages — not just on the base class. Also fixed `if-id-tagline`'s hide‑threshold (native "medium" was hiding it at ≤991, the embed's unconditional `display:flex!important` + its own ≤575 hide were the ONLY reason it stayed visible down to a sensible width — replaced with a clean native "small" (≤767) override, nearest‑native‑breakpoint‑erring‑earlier per HARD RULE #2). `if-id-search`/`if-navbtn`'s embed rules were confirmed fully redundant with existing native overrides — removed with no native change needed. Added native `if-umdbar-in`/`if-umdbar-sep` "small"/"tiny" breakpoints (500/380 mapped to the nearest native tiers, erring wider per HARD RULE #2) and native `white-space:nowrap` on `if-umdbar-a1`/`-a2`.
- **Removed all four now‑redundant blocks from the embed** (`set_settings`), cutting it from ~9KB to ~5KB. **Deliberately LEFT IN**: the hamburger→✕ morph (`.if-bar`/`.w--open` transform+opacity — genuinely parent‑state‑driven, would need verifying `.w--open` combo support before migrating, not attempted this session) and the entire mobile‑menu block (`.if-navmenu` at ≤991, ~100 lines, tightly coupled to the JS‑inserted `.if-mtext` wrapper) — both flagged as remaining candidates for a future native migration, not migrated now given the risk of breaking primary navigation under time pressure vs. the lower payoff (neither was causing an active visual bug).
- **Systematically scanned the ENTIRE shared compiled CSS (one file, covers all 4 pages) for the SAME "combo has no breakpoint override while its base class does" pattern** that caused the hero bug — wrote a script that pairs every `.base.combo{}` rule against `.base{}`'s own media‑query overrides for layout‑critical properties (`grid-template-columns/-rows`, `flex-direction`, `display`, `width`, `max-width`, `min-width`). Validated the script first against the PRE‑fix CSS (correctly flagged the known hero‑inner bug twice, once per breakpoint) before trusting a **zero‑results** run against the POST‑fix CSS — no other instance of this exact pattern exists site‑wide for these properties.
- **Verified end‑to‑end:** published, re‑fetched the live compiled CSS, rebuilt the offline harness from THAT fresh CSS (not a stale copy), and swept the real About‑page hero across 320–1440px — confirmed 2‑column layout with the correct `1.15fr/.85fr` ratio at 800–991px, single‑column at ≤767px, and `h1Overflow` negative (no overflow) at every single width tested. Screenshot at 390px shows a clean single‑column stack, matching the user's reported scenario exactly. Confirmed Students and Faculty carry the identical `if-hero-grid if-hero-inner` classes, so the same fix applies to them automatically (shared combo, no per‑page work needed).
- **Scope note (deliberately not silently omitted):** this pass covered the hero grid + header visibility + UMD bar — the concrete bug plus everything the systematic scan could verify was governed by the SAME kind of rule. It did **not** attempt the hamburger‑morph or mobile‑menu blocks (see above) or re‑examine every OTHER property type beyond the layout‑critical list scanned. If another "looks fine in the Designer but wrong when a combo/breakpoint combination is hit" bug surfaces, re‑run the same base‑vs‑combo scan with the property list widened to whatever's implicated.

## 55. Mobile‑menu block ALSO migrated off custom code (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

Same session as §54, continued per explicit user instruction to finish the job rather than leave it flagged. Migrated essentially the ENTIRE `.if-navmenu` (≤991) mobile‑dropdown‑menu block from the shared `st0` embed to native "medium"‑breakpoint Designer styles: `if-navmenu` (border/shadow/padding), `if-navbar-in::after` (the gold corner accent, using the native `after` pseudo — Webflow's pseudo enum supports `before`/`after`, so this needed no code at all), `if-nav-link`/`if-ddtoggle`/`if-nav-sublink` hover states (native `hover` pseudo scoped to the `medium` breakpoint achieves the same "mobile‑only hover style" the embed's ancestor‑selector was doing), `if-dd-wrap`/`if-dd-list` (position/opacity/overflow/padding), `if-mtoprow` (order/border/padding), `if-give-mobile` (display:none), `if-msearch` (background‑image search icon + focus state), and `if-give-btn-solid` (medium display/order/colors + hover).
- **Caught a real, significant mismatch while re‑checking, not just cosmetic drift:** the mobile GIVE button's EXISTING native "medium" style was `display:none` with a red‑outline look — completely wrong. The embed's `!important` override was ACTUALLY making it `display:block` with a solid gold fill, which is what's been live the whole time. If the embed had been deleted without this specific fix, the mobile GIVE button would have silently vanished. Similarly caught `padding-right` mismatches (native said 24px, embed's `!important` said 40px — the extra width was reserved for the gold corner accent) on `if-nav-link`/`if-ddtoggle`/`if-nav-sublink`. **Lesson: when a class has an EXISTING native breakpoint override that looks superficially plausible, verify it AGAINST the embed's actual values property‑by‑property — don't assume "already has a medium override" means "already correct."**
- **Deliberately LEFT IN the embed** (per explicit user instruction — "the hamburger was never a problem… things that will never be touched"): the hamburger→✕ transform morph (`.w--open .if-bar:nth-child(N)`) and the `.if-mtext` text‑grow‑on‑hover mechanism (base styling + its `:hover .if-mtext{scale}` parent‑hover‑child rules + the JS that inserts the `.if-mtext` wrapper span). Both are genuinely non‑native: Webflow's Designer has no selector for "when an ANCESTOR has class/state X, style this element" (no `:has()`, no ancestor combinator in the style panel), so a parent's hover/open‑state driving a *different* element's transform can't be expressed natively — this is the same class of exception HARD RULE #0 already carves out for parent‑hover‑child. **The distinction the user drew: code that implements interaction BEHAVIOR nobody edits via content changes is fine to stay as code; code that determines VISIBLE APPEARANCE/layout that must look right and be editable is not.** Apply that distinction before deciding what's in-scope for a "get this off custom code" pass — don't try to eliminate every line of an embed indiscriminately.
- Embed cut from ~5KB to ~1.6KB (hamburger + mtext + the wrapper script only).

## 56. ⚠️⚠️ CSS Grid + `display:contents` + large negative offset = a WEBFLOW‑DESIGNER‑ONLY rendering breakage (2026‑07‑09) — reverted to flexbox, native Designer, PUBLISHED

**What happened:** §53 restructured the stage‑header cluster (glyph/eyebrow/title/watermark) from a flex‑column into a 2‑row CSS Grid + `display:contents` specifically to make the glyph‑to‑title baseline lock survive eyebrow line‑wrapping without JS. It was verified correct in a real browser (Playwright/Chromium) AND got one successful Designer snapshot right after building it. Session continued into other work (§54/§55's embed migration). When the user later looked at the About page's stage‑header bars directly in the Designer, at every breakpoint, the sections showed a MASSIVE phantom‑empty color pool (many screen‑heights of solid background, nothing to scroll to) and the glyph image was **not visible anywhere** — while the PUBLISHED page (confirmed independently by the user, not just by me) looked completely correct.
- **Root cause (best‑supported theory, not proven with certainty — see the tooling caveat below):** the combination of (a) `display:contents` dissolving `if-stage-head-txt` so its children become direct grid items of `if-stage-head-row`, (b) explicit `grid-row` placement on those children AND on the glyph/watermark, and (c) a `top`/`margin-top` offset that's a large **negative** `calc()` value (needed to pull the glyph/watermark up into position) — is something the Webflow **Designer's own canvas rendering pipeline** mishandles, even though the identical CSS renders perfectly in real Chromium (both in an offline Playwright harness AND on the actual published site). This is the SAME family of bug as §28/§31E/§34 ("Designer inflates/mis-sizes elements a real browser handles fine") but a NEW trigger combination (grid + contents + large negative offset) not previously catalogued.
- **⚠️ Verification gap that let this ship in the first place:** after building §53, verification leaned on (1) the real‑browser offline harness and (2) ONE successful Designer snapshot taken before moving on to unrelated work. Neither caught this because: the harness is a real browser (doesn't reproduce a Designer‑only bug by definition), and the one successful snapshot didn't get re‑checked after later edits (§51‑style sizing tweaks layered on top). **`element_snapshot_tool` also went into a period of general failure this session** (confirmed by testing an unrelated, untouched Home‑page element — same generic `status:false` error — so at least part of the difficulty verifying this was an unrelated tool outage, not something to over‑index on either). **Lesson: a single passing Designer snapshot right after a change is not durable proof — re‑verify the Designer specifically after ANY further edit to the same elements, and treat tool outages as a real possibility rather than assuming a failing verification call means the content itself is broken.**
- **Fix — reverted §53 entirely, back to the pre‑§53 flex‑column structure**, keeping §51's sizing/window‑extension work (which was independently good) intact: `if-stage-head-txt` back to `display:flex;flex-direction:column` (own `grid-row-gap` restored); `if-stage-eyebrow-row`/`if-stage-title` lost their `grid-column`/`grid-row`/`align-self` (not grid items anymore); `if-stage-glyphimg`/`if-glyph-tall`/`if-glyph-line` lost `top`/`grid-column`/`grid-row`/`align-self`, `margin-top` restored to the FULL §45‑era formula (`eyebrowFontTerm + headtxtgapTerm + titleTerm + 20.25px − ownHeightTerm`, using the §51‑extended clamps for the gap/height terms); `if-stage-wm` similarly restored, losing its grid placement. **This reintroduces the narrower, pre‑existing eyebrow‑wrap baseline discontinuity (§45b, JS‑compensated on the published site, ~±17px in the Designer only when an eyebrow wraps) — a known, much smaller trade accepted deliberately in exchange for eliminating the far worse multi‑screen/invisible‑glyph breakage.**
- **Also converted `if-stage-head-row` from `display:grid` (`grid-template-columns:auto 1fr`, pre‑existing since §31H/45, unrelated to §53) to `display:flex` (`column-gap` + `align-items:start`, glyph auto‑width + `if-stage-head-txt` given `flex-grow:1;flex-shrink:1;flex-basis:0%` to fill the remainder)** — per an explicit, standing user preference stated this session: **"I DO NOT LIKE WEBFLOW GRID, I prefer flexbox when at all possible."** Going forward, default to flexbox for any new layout; only reach for CSS Grid when a layout genuinely needs it (e.g. true 2‑D placement) and even then, weigh this session's Designer‑breakage finding before doing so.
- **Verified (offline, real published compiled CSS + real Interstate + real cropped glyph SVGs, ALL 4 stages, 320–1440px, 17 widths):** every section height is sane (335–531px — NOT multi‑screen), **glyph visible at 100% of width/stage combinations tested (0 invisible)**, 0 page overflow, 0 JS errors, baseline delta back to the expected §45b pattern (≈0 where eyebrow is 1 line, ~±17px only in the narrow ranges where it wraps — matching pre‑§53 behavior, not a regression from this revert). **Could NOT get a fresh Designer‑canvas snapshot to confirm visually** — `element_snapshot_tool` was in a general failure state for this entire verification pass (confirmed via an unrelated Home‑page element, same error) — flagging this honestly rather than claiming a Designer‑verified "done" without one. **Ask the next session (or the user directly) to confirm via the Designer once the tool is healthy again; if it still looks wrong, the grid/contents/negative‑offset theory above is where to keep digging, but the flexbox‑based revert is the most defensible starting point given everything actually checked out clean.**
- **Standing lesson for this whole project, stated plainly:** CSS Grid, `display:contents`, and large‑magnitude negative offsets are now each individually flagged as "verify the ACTUAL Designer canvas after every single edit, not just once" — the failure mode here wasn't using non‑native CSS (everything used was 100% standard, real, native‑settable CSS) — it was that a **real, valid, standard CSS combination** happened to trigger a **Webflow‑Designer‑specific** rendering bug that no amount of real‑browser/published verification could have caught. The only defense is: re‑check the Designer, specifically, after every edit to the same elements — not just once after the first build.

## 57. Stage‑header eyebrow/title — phone‑only shrink to match glyph + eliminate eyebrow line‑wrap (2026‑07‑09) — native "small" (≤767) breakpoint override, PUBLISHED

Two related user asks, fixed together. (1) After §51's glyph‑size reduction at phone widths, the eyebrow/title text was left at its old (§45 ×1.5) size, so text no longer matched the now‑smaller glyph — user: *"Text needs to shrink to match glyph size. This only appears to be an issue on the smaller (phone-sized) breakpoints."* (2) Mid‑fix, user flagged (with a "STAGE 03 · ASSEMBLY LINE" screenshot) that this eyebrow line must never wrap — a wrap pushes the title down, breaking the §31H/§45/§53 glyph‑to‑title baseline lock — and asked to **shrink that line specifically on phone views** to prevent it.

- **⚠️ FIRST ATTEMPT WAS WRONG SCOPE — caught before shipping.** Initially re‑derived `if-stage-eyebrow`/`if-stage-title` font‑size as ONE continuous clamp on `[320,1440]` (mirroring §51's glyph treatment). Checked the result and found it ALSO shrank the eyebrow at tablet widths (e.g. 15.09px vs the original 18.25px at 991px) — but BOTH user asks explicitly said "phone‑sized … ONLY" / "only … on the smaller (phone‑sized) breakpoints." A continuous whole‑range clamp was the wrong tool here (correct for the glyph in §51, since nobody flagged tablet‑range glyph size as wrong; wrong for text, which the user explicitly scoped to phones). **Lesson: "shrink at the low end" does not automatically mean "one continuous clamp from 320 to 1440" — check whether the ask is scoped to a sub‑range, and if so use a native breakpoint override for just that sub‑range, leaving the wider‑range formula untouched.**
- **Fix — added a native `small` (≤767) breakpoint override**, leaving the existing `main` breakpoint formula on `if-stage-eyebrow` (font‑size, letter‑spacing) and `if-stage-title` (font‑size) **completely untouched** (so tablet/desktop are byte‑identical to before). The small‑breakpoint clamp's window is `[320,767]`, with its value AT 767 solved to exactly match the (untouched) main formula's own value at 767 — guaranteeing zero visible jump crossing the 767 boundary:
  - `if-stage-eyebrow` font‑size (small): `clamp(8.5px, calc(1.96346px + 2.04267vw), 17.63073px)` (17.63073 = the main formula's own value at w=767).
  - `if-stage-eyebrow` letter‑spacing (small): `clamp(0.1px, calc(-3.68182px + 1.18182vw), .14em)` — kept the ceiling as the literal `.14em` (not a pre‑computed px value) so it always tracks whatever the small‑breakpoint font‑size is at that width; the calc's slope is deliberately steep enough that it already exceeds `.14em` by ~650px, so it's flat/saturated to the unchanged `.14em` look for the top third of the small range.
  - `if-stage-title` font‑size (small): `clamp(30px, calc(11.16816px + 5.88495vw), 56.30573px)`.
  - The embedded `titleTerm` (0.85×title‑font, used inside the glyph `margin-top`/watermark `top` baseline‑lock formulas) got its OWN matching small‑breakpoint value: `clamp(25.5px, calc(9.49294px + 5.00221vw), 47.85987px)`.
  - `if-stage-glyphimg` / `if-glyph-tall` / `if-glyph-line` `margin-top` and `if-stage-wm` `top` all got small‑breakpoint overrides rebuilding the SAME baseline‑lock formula (`eyebrowFontTerm + headtxtgapTerm + titleTerm + 20.25px − ownHeightTerm`) with the new small‑range eyebrow/title terms substituted in, keeping `headtxtgapTerm` and `ownHeightTerm` as the SAME continuous `[320,1440]` formulas already in place from §51 (unaffected, not re‑scoped).
- **Floor values (8.5px eyebrow font, 0.1px letter‑spacing floor, 30px title font) were derived empirically, not guessed.** Measured the TRUE intrinsic (non‑flex‑shrunk) width of each stage's actual eyebrow string — via a detached, absolutely‑positioned clone with `white-space:nowrap`, since a naive `getBoundingClientRect()` on the live flex item just reports its flex‑shrunk box, not its content width — against the real available column width at each candidate width, for all 4 stage names. **"STAGE 03 · ASSEMBLY LINE" is the longest/tightest string at every width** (confirms the user's own example was the worst case). Iterated candidate (font floor, letter‑spacing floor) pairs through the real offline harness until zero wraps remained down to 320px for all 4 stages, with a small (~6%) margin at the tightest case rather than a razor's‑edge fit.
- **⚠️ Second bug caught mid‑build: initial offline test patch put the override EARLIER in the CSS file than the original unconditional rule** (inserted into the first `@media (max-width:767px)` block found, which turned out to be Webflow's early framework grid‑utility block, not the later block holding the real `.if-stage-eyebrow` base rule at position ~87688). Since equal‑specificity cascade ties break on source order regardless of which rule is media‑scoped, the later unconditional rule kept winning even at ≤767px — the override appeared to do nothing. **Fixed the TEST HARNESS by appending a brand‑new media block at the end of the file** (guaranteed last in source order). This was purely a hand‑patching artifact for offline testing — Webflow's own `update_style` with `breakpoint_id:"small"` places small‑breakpoint rules correctly (confirmed: verified post‑publish that Webflow emitted the base rule at 87688 and the small‑breakpoint override at 107833, properly nested inside the SAME real `@media screen and (max-width: 767px)` block already used by every other small‑breakpoint style on the page). **Lesson: when hand‑patching a compiled stylesheet for an offline test, a media‑scoped override must still be LATER in the file than the unconditional rule it's meant to beat, or equal‑specificity source‑order rules make it lose anyway — this is not a concern with the real `update_style` tool, which orders breakpoints correctly, but it can silently invalidate a hand‑built verification harness.**
- **Verified (offline, both WITH and WITHOUT the shared JS loaded — i.e. published‑equivalent AND Designer‑equivalent):** real Interstate woff2 + real cropped glyph SVGs + real per‑stage eyebrow/title copy, 16 widths from 320–1440, all 4 stages. Zero wraps anywhere in the range; glyph‑to‑title baseline delta stays ≤1px throughout, WITH OR WITHOUT JS (confirming the fix is genuinely native — the §45b JS wrap‑compensation module is now an inert no‑op for this page, since the wrap it existed to compensate for no longer happens); values above 767px are byte‑identical to the pre‑fix formula (18.25 at 991, 18.83 at 1200, 19.5 ceiling at ≥1440 — zero tablet/desktop change). **Re‑verified against the ACTUAL live published HTML+CSS after publishing** (re‑curled fresh, confirmed Webflow's own compiled output matches the intended formulas exactly) — same zero‑wrap, zero‑tablet‑change result. **Verified the Designer canvas directly via `element_snapshot_tool`** on the stage‑03 section (main/default breakpoint) — renders correctly, matching the known‑good look, confirming no regression from this change (the tool doesn't expose a way to force a specific breakpoint remotely, so the phone‑width Designer state relies on the native‑CSS‑only harness verification above, which is authoritative since no JS is involved in either surface).
- **Dials:** the four numbers to nudge if ever revisited — eyebrow font floor (8.5), letter‑spacing floor (0.1), title font floor (30), and the letter‑spacing taper's saturation point (650px, i.e. how far into the small range it stays tapering vs. flat). All four live only in the `small`‑breakpoint override, so nudging them can never again touch tablet/desktop.

## 58. ASPIRE spinoff staging — duplicate page + INDEPENDENT nav component (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, unpublished/draft

User is developing a small spinoff site, **ASPIRE**, using this master site as the dev repo (the standing multi‑site model, §4). Their plan: stage the content here → **Duplicate Site** for the actual ASPIRE spinoff → edit the nav on the copy → delete the spinoff's other pages. This entry documents the staging setup.

- **New page: `ASPIRE-Home`** — id `6a4f46535dfa231a17dd7b3c`, slug `/aspire-home`, `draft:true` (excluded from publish/crawl, same convention as the Library page, §6). Created via `create_page` with `duplicateOf` the Home page (`6a316b1d0f02a4cda75a51fb`) — full content clone. **Deliberately NOT linked from Main Nav** (no link added anywhere; user's explicit instruction).
- **⚠️ Why a plain page‑duplicate wasn't enough — component sharing.** Home's header uses the **`Main Nav`** component (`bffe20a1‑a591‑82e2‑1c75‑4864d9f1f0b9`, group "Chrome", regular per‑site component, §12) as a **ComponentInstance**. Duplicating the PAGE alone would have put ANOTHER instance of that SAME shared component on ASPIRE‑Home — editing "the nav" there would have edited Home/Students/About/Faculty's nav too (component defs are shared within one Webflow site; only a full **Duplicate Site** severs that link). That's exactly what the user asked to avoid ("a duplicate instance... I can edit there WITHOUT it affecting the MAIN NAV on the normal pages").
- **Fix — genuinely independent component, swapped in on ASPIRE‑Home only:**
  1. `duplicate_component` on `Main Nav` → new component **`Main Nav — ASPIRE`**, id `c2a30d5f‑30b2‑2b97‑a18d‑49f791856b25`, group **"ASPIRE"** (kept out of "Chrome" so it doesn't clutter the master site's real chrome components list). A duplicated component is a fully separate definition — editing either one never touches the other, even while both live in this one site.
  2. Inserted an instance of `Main Nav — ASPIRE` into ASPIRE‑Home's `if-header` block, positioned `before` the `st0` layout embed (`afd96462…`) — preserves the original child order (UMD Bar → Nav → embed). **`insert_component_instance` cannot anchor `before/after` on a component‑instance element** (errors: "Cannot insert relative to a component instance") — anchored on the plain HtmlEmbed sibling instead.
  3. Removed the old shared `Main Nav` instance from ASPIRE‑Home.
  - **Verified structurally:** `if-header` on ASPIRE‑Home now reads `UMD Bar → Main Nav — ASPIRE → HtmlEmbed` (queried post‑swap); `Main Nav`'s `instanceCount` confirmed back to **4** (Home/Students/About/Faculty only — ASPIRE‑Home no longer counted). **`element_snapshot_tool` was down for this whole check** (identical generic failure on a known‑good, untouched Home‑page element too — a tool‑wide outage, not a content problem, same pattern as §56) — visual Designer confirmation is still owed once the tool recovers; the structural query result is unambiguous in the meantime.
- **⚠️ Scope of the isolation — component vs. shared classes.** `duplicate_component` isolates STRUCTURE (which links exist, their text/hrefs, dropdown contents) — that's what "editing the nav without affecting the main nav" means here, and it's fully isolated now. It does **not** isolate the **Designer style classes** the nav's elements still reference (`if-nav-link`, `if-dd-wrap`, `if-navmenu`, etc. — global, site‑wide, per HARD RULE re: `if-` classes). Editing one of THOSE classes right now would still restyle the real Main Nav too, same as any other `if-` class edit. This is expected/harmless for now (nothing has been styled yet) and becomes fully moot the moment the user runs **Duplicate Site** for the actual ASPIRE spinoff — that clones the classes too, making the spinoff's copy 100% independent at every level (§4). If the user wants to style `Main Nav — ASPIRE` distinctly BEFORE duplicating the site, give its elements new non‑shared classes rather than editing the existing `if-` ones.
- **Nothing published/promoted** — page is draft, component is unused elsewhere; no shared‑file (`idea-factory.{css,js}`) change. `stable` unaffected.
- **Hand‑off (user's plan, unblocked):** Webflow **Duplicate Site** → on the copy, edit `Main Nav — ASPIRE`'s links/menu for the real ASPIRE structure → delete the copy's other pages (Home/Students/About/Faculty/Library/404/401) → keep only ASPIRE‑Home (rename/repoint as desired) → add the new domain to the Adobe Fonts kit allow‑list (§6 step 2, the one per‑spinoff step Duplicate Site can't do) → publish.

## 59. ASPIRE spinoff — header "program logo" block replacing the tagline (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer + 1 uploaded asset, PUBLISHED, ASPIRE-Home only

On spinoff sites, the header white bar's tagline (`.if-id-tagline`, "The University of Maryland's home for…") is replaced by a **program identity block**: program name (1–3 lines) + gold accent + a small stage‑glyph badge with a caption. Built inside the **independent `Main Nav — ASPIRE` component** (§58) — the master's real `Main Nav`/tagline is completely untouched (confirmed: its instance count stayed at 4 throughout this work).

**⚠️ RENAMED (2026‑07‑09, see CONVENTIONS §3): every `if-hdrprog*` class named below was renamed to `program-page-aspire-hdrprog*`** (e.g. `if-hdrprog`→`program-page-aspire-hdrprog`, `if-hdrprog-title`→`program-page-aspire-hdrprog-title`, etc.) per the new per‑program‑page naming protocol. The structure/values described in §59/§59a are otherwise unchanged — mentally substitute the prefix when reading. `if-hdrprog-sep` (the divider) was deleted outright, not renamed — see §59b.

**Structure (children of `if-id-tagline`, which keeps its own shared style untouched — only its CONTENTS were swapped):**
```
div.if-hdrprog
  div.if-hdrprog-name
    p.if-hdrprog-title      "ASPIRE Program"
    div.if-hdrprog-bar      (gold accent, normal-flow — not absolute, per the empty-div-inflation lesson, §28/§31E)
  div.if-hdrprog-sep        (1px vertical divider)
  div.if-hdrprog-badge
    img.if-hdrprog-glyph    (small stage glyph)
    p.if-hdrprog-label      "BLUEPRINT"
```
New global classes: `if-hdrprog, if-hdrprog-name, if-hdrprog-title, if-hdrprog-bar, if-hdrprog-sep, if-hdrprog-badge, if-hdrprog-glyph, if-hdrprog-label`. None of these are shared with any master-site class — safe to restyle freely without affecting Home/Students/About/Faculty.

**Glyph asset — needed a NEW upload, the existing one was the wrong variant.** The About page's stage-header glyphs (§30/§31I) are recolored **white-on-transparent**, made for sitting on a *colored* header bar (red/maroon/black/gold). This header bar is **white**, so the white glyph was invisible (white-on-white) until swapped for a **red-on-transparent, cropped** variant — uploaded as a new asset (`stage-01-blueprint-red-crop.svg`, id `6a4f4bfd19b3a64ddd8de017`, viewBox `26 26 68 68` matching the same crop as the About page's red-on-header glyphs). **Lesson: the site's 3 existing glyph families (plain colored/transparent, white-on-color/transparent, white-tile-with-bg) each target a DIFFERENT background — check which one actually matches the surface before reusing an asset id; "it's the same glyph" isn't enough, the recolor variant has to match the bg it sits on.**

**Verification method:** `ASPIRE-Home` is a **draft** page (§58) so it's never served at its slug — `curl`-based verification of the live HTML doesn't work here. Instead: (1) `get_all_elements`/`query_elements` scoped to the component confirm exact DOM structure + applied classes; (2) the compiled CSS IS served regardless of draft status (one shared stylesheet for the whole site), so `curl`ing any published page's CSS `<link>` and grepping for the new class names confirms the real, live property values; (3) hand-assembled an offline harness mirroring the queried DOM exactly, using the real compiled CSS + real Interstate woff2 + the real uploaded glyph fetched from the CDN + the real inline logo SVG (copied verbatim from the queried DOM), rendered via the same Playwright pipeline used throughout this project. This is the reusable pattern for verifying anything on a **draft** page going forward — direct HTML curl won't work, but element-tree query + shared-CSS curl + hand-assembled harness does.

### 59a. Alignment refinement — glyph anchored to the LOGO baseline, bar/label share one bottom edge at every line-count (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED

User: "we have to have some stylistic coherence" (stated twice) — align the glyph's bottom mini-square row with the baseline of the "F" in the **logo's** "Factory" wordmark (a fixed reference, independent of program-name length — NOT the About-page pattern of anchoring to the title's own first line), make the gold bar fall in line with the glyph's caption label, and tighten the title's line-height so multi-line names hold together.

- **Key structural insight: don't compute a per-line-count position — bottom-anchor everything to ONE shared edge and let flexbox handle the rest.** `if-hdrprog` got `align-self:flex-end` (bottom-anchors it against `if-id-tagline`'s full stretched height, 95px, driven by the logo — the tallest sibling in `if-id-band`'s `align-items:stretch` row) **and** its own `align-items` changed `center`→`flex-end` (bottom-aligns its two children, `if-hdrprog-name` and `if-hdrprog-badge`, to that SAME edge). Because both columns' bottoms land on the identical edge regardless of their own height, the gold bar (last element in the name column) and the label (last element in the badge column) coincide automatically at 1, 2, or 3 title lines — **no per-line-count logic needed**, confirmed identical (94px from band top) across all three measured cases.
- **The logo-baseline anchor was solved algebraically, not eyeballed.** Measured (real render): band height 95px; "F" path's rendered bottom (baseline) = 53.5px from band top; badge's glyph height 30px, label height 20px. With the badge bottom-anchored to 95, `glyphBottom = 95 − gap − labelHeight`; solving `95 − gap − 20 = 53.5` gives `gap = 21.5px` — so **the fix was raising `if-hdrprog-badge`'s `grid-row-gap` from 4px to 21.5px**, nothing else. Verified post-publish against the real compiled CSS: glyph bottom lands at 52.5px (≈1px off the 53.5px target — imperceptible, within font-metric rounding).
- **`if-hdrprog-glyph` width changed from a fixed `30px` to `auto`** (height stays a fixed `30px`) — makes the glyph shape-agnostic. The user flagged that a glyph can be "one to three rows high" (different stage glyphs have different width:height ratios — the About page's "assembly line" glyph is a wide short rectangle, not a square) — fixing height and letting width follow the asset's own aspect ratio prevents a non-square glyph from being squished, without needing to know in advance which glyph a given program will use.
- **`if-hdrprog-title` `line-height` 1.2 → 1.08** — tightens multi-line names into one visually held-together block. Checked for descender clipping (e.g. "Entrepreneurship"'s "p") at all 3 test lengths — none.
- **⚠️ Known limitation, not fixed this pass (deliberately deferred — no program uses this glyph shape yet):** the gap-based math above assumes the glyph's own bottom-ROW is flush with the glyph IMAGE's bottom edge (true for the Blueprint 2×2 and the Scale-Up 3-row/staircase crops). The About page's "Assembly Line" glyph type is a box-row **plus a belt hanging below it** (§31G/§45's `frac=0.667` correction) — if a future program uses that glyph here, the same `frac` adjustment from the About page stage-header system would need to be re-applied (effectively treat the glyph's usable height as `0.667×renderedHeight` when solving the gap), or the math will place the *belt* at the F-baseline instead of the box-row. Flag this if/when that glyph type is ever used in this header context.
- **Verified via the same draft-page harness technique as §59** (real compiled CSS re-curled post-publish, real assets, real logo markup) — bar-bottom = label-bottom = 94px at all 3 line-counts; glyph-bottom ≈ F-baseline (52.5 vs 53.5); row height stable at 95px throughout; 0 overflow. `element_snapshot_tool` was down for the whole verification pass (confirmed via both a wrong-scope retry AND a same-scope retry, both generic failures) — flagged rather than silently skipped; structural correctness independently confirmed via `query_elements` (the element exists, right styles applied) and the compiled-CSS-based harness stood in for the visual Designer check.
- **Option B (the alternative "cohesive icon+eyebrow" layout proposed alongside Option A) was NOT touched this pass** — user's feedback was specifically about Option A (the screenshot they annotated), so all fixes above apply only to the currently-live Option A structure. Option B remains an unbuilt mockup pending a decision.

## 60. ASPIRE header block — precise sizing spec (glyph/label/title) + hero photo swap + naming protocol (2026‑07‑09) — native Designer, PUBLISHED, in progress

Continuation of §59/§59a, refining the Option A program‑logo block with an exact typographic spec instead of eyeballed values, plus a hero‑image swap. **Mid‑pass, the user introduced a standing naming protocol — see CONVENTIONS §3 — and all `if-hdrprog*` classes were renamed to `program-page-aspire-hdrprog*`.** The rest of this entry uses the NEW names.

- **Hero photo swapped** (`if-hero-photo`, element `8bc56d37‑78c2‑d2cb‑0fae‑d426e0747536`): was the generic `building-flare.png` (`6a336dff…`, shared with other pages) → now the ASPIRE‑specific `aspire-01-hero.png` (asset `6a4554a17c599971ed8ac298`) — a photo of two students in safety glasses assembling a robotic arm. **The asset already existed in the site's library** (uploaded previously, unused) — confirmed via `get_asset_preview` before applying, per the user's explicit instruction not to re‑upload/duplicate it. Alt text updated to match. **Other pre‑staged, still‑unused ASPIRE section photos found in the library for later sections:** `aspire-02-about.png`, `aspire-03-band.png` (proving‑ground/field‑testing), `aspire-04-mentors.png` (assembly‑line/incubator), `aspire-05-contact.png`.
- **"The top right rectangle" — CONFIRMED by the user (not guessed) = the thin red horizontal bar at the very top of the icon's right‑hand window shape** (`<rect x="278.9" y="10" width="304" height="28.3" fill="#e11e34">` in the logo SVG) — i.e. "Candidate B" in the comparison render, NOT the wide gold block ("Candidate A") initially assumed more likely. **Lesson: when a spatial description has two plausible referents in a compound graphic, render BOTH candidates outlined/labeled and ask — do not commit to the more "visually obvious" one on assumption; the user's own mental model of their asset's construction can differ from an outside reading of the raw shapes.**
- **`program-page-aspire-hdrprog-bar` (the gold underline) reshaped to match that reference rectangle's PLAIN shape:** removed the angled `clip-path` (was borrowed from the hero/stage‑header "cut corner" convention) — the reference rectangle has square, unclipped edges, so the underline now does too. Kept `width:100%` (of the 260px name column), `height:4px`, `background:#ffd200`.
- **Divider removed:** `if-hdrprog-sep` (renamed target abandoned — deleted outright, element + style) per the user: "remove the vertical grey line between the program name and the glyph — this is meant to read as pertaining to one entity."
- **Precise sizing spec, measured (not eyeballed) — user's 3‑part instruction:**
  1. *Glyph mini‑square height = the logo's "F" (in "Factory") height.* Measured via `getBoundingClientRect` on the actual SVG path (the "F" is the 5th `<path>` in the logo, 0‑indexed 4 — paths run I‑d‑e‑a (upper line) then F‑a‑c‑t‑o‑r‑y (lower line), confirmed by their y‑ranges): **F height = 14.94px**. The Blueprint crop's mini‑squares are `30/68 ≈ 44.12%` of the crop's own total height (viewBox `26 26 68 68`) → target glyph display size = `14.94 / 0.4412 ≈ 33.87px` (square, since this crop is 1:1).
  2. *Label ("BLUEPRINT") letter‑height = the logo's lowercase "r" height.* "r" = the 10th path (0‑indexed 9) = **11.44px** measured the same way. Since the label renders `text-transform:uppercase`, its CAP height (not x‑height) must equal 11.44px — calibrated Interstate Bold's own cap‑height ratio empirically via Canvas `measureText().actualBoundingBoxAscent/Descent` on a real capital letter at a reference size (**66% of font‑size**, confirmed weight‑independent 700 vs 800). Solved target font‑size = `11.44 / 0.66 ≈ 17.33px`. Also set per the user: color `#7f7f7f` (unchanged), `Interstate` (unchanged), **weight 700 "Bold"** (unchanged — was already 700, distinguishing it from the title's heavier 800), **tracking → 0** (was `0.08em`).
  3. *Program title = Interstate 800 (the heaviest weight the Adobe kit loads — confirmed no heavier exists, per §22 — so 800 already satisfies "heaviest available," no faux‑bold), tracking `-15` (Adobe/Illustrator‑convention thousandths‑of‑an‑em → `letter-spacing:-0.015em`), leading = the same value as the font‑size → `line-height:1` (unitless — this literally means leading equals font‑size in CSS, no extra interpretation needed).
- **⚠️ NOT YET APPLIED TO WEBFLOW — blocked on a genuine open question, tested only in the offline harness.** Point 5 of the spec ("center vertically the glyph+label, and the title+bar, [at the same level as] the top‑right rectangle") requires computing each group's vertical CENTER to match rect8's own center (measured at **20.83px from the row top**, in the current 95px‑tall row). **Built and measured this in the harness first** (position:relative + top:20.83px + transform:translateY(‑50%) on each of the two flex‑children, independent of their own content height — the standard CSS "center on an arbitrary target Y regardless of element height" technique): confirms both groups' centers land exactly on the target for all 3 line‑counts, **but the badge and the 3‑line title case then visually overflow ABOVE the current 95px row (into the black UMD bar)** by up to ~6.5px, since rect8 sits high in the row and the content needs room both above and below its center. **Asked the user (via AskUserQuestion) whether to let the row grow taller to fit, keep it fixed and accept the clipping, or reconsider the target — the question was dismissed ("wait for next instruction") without an answer on the overflow handling specifically**, though the user DID separately clarify the rectangle identity via plain text (rect8, confirmed above) and gave the underline‑reshape instruction (applied). **The overflow question remains open — do not apply the centering/sizing values above to Webflow until it's resolved**, to avoid shipping a native change that clips into the header bar above.
- New naming note: any further classes for this feature must use the `program-page-aspire-` prefix (CONVENTIONS §3) — `program-page-aspire-hdrprog`, `-name`, `-title`, `-bar`, `-badge`, `-glyph`, `-label` are the current 7 (the divider was deleted, not renamed).
